Srestrict vault reference resolution to schema-marked sensitive fields (swamp-club#2119) (#2446)

Security-related change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-14T00:28:10Zsecurityfix

What's Changed

  • fix(security): restrict vault reference resolution to schema-marked sensitive fields (swamp-club#2119) (#2446)

Summary

  • Fixes a security vulnerability where readResource and data query paths resolved vault reference strings (${{ vault.get(...) }}) in ALL fields, not just those the schema marks sensitive
  • Attacker-controlled text (PR titles, commit messages, ticket descriptions) matching the vault.get pattern was silently resolved to the actual secret value
  • Introduces resolveSensitiveVaultRefs — a path-targeted resolver that only resolves vault references at specified sensitive field dot-paths
  • Stores vaulted field paths as a _swamp.sensitiveFields tag on data records at write time; all six read sites parse this tag

Changes

| File | Change |

|------|--------|

| src/domain/models/data_writer.ts | New resolveSensitiveVaultRefs, parseSensitiveFieldsTag, parseSensitiveFieldsFromRowTags; processSensitiveResourceData returns vaulted paths; createResourceReader uses tag + schema fallback |

| src/domain/models/in_process_executor.ts | Pass resource specs to createResourceReader |

| src/worker/remote_method_context.ts | Use findByName for tags, path-targeted resolution |

| src/domain/data/data_query_service.ts | Parse _swamp.sensitiveFields from CatalogRow tags |

| src/domain/data/data_record_mapper.ts | Parse tag from Data entity/handle tags |

| src/domain/expressions/model_resolver.ts | Parse tag from data entity

𝗗 release v20260914.002634.0-sha.bbd795f7𝗖𝗟 bbd795f7𝗣 #2446

Sstrip SWAMP_* env vars from shell model child processes (#2435)

Security-related change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-11T21:51:00Zsecurityfix

What's Changed

  • fix(security): strip SWAMP_* env vars from shell model child processes (#2435)

Summary

  • Add createSafeMethodEnv() to environment_snapshot.ts — strips all SWAMP_* variables from the process env before spawning method children, with per-variable allowlist for opt-in
  • Shell model now builds a safe env (createSafeMethodEnv + clearEnv: true) so external tools never inherit swamp auth tokens
  • Add clearEnv option to ProcessExecutorOptions to prevent Deno's default env augmentation from re-leaking stripped vars
  • Document the third env scrubbing boundary in design/enablers/remote-execution.md

Context

Methods running on the loopback executor inherited the full parent environment when spawning child processes, leaking auth tokens like SWAMP_SERVER_TOKEN to external tools (LLM CLIs, kubectl, etc.). The remote execution layer already had env scrubbing at two boundaries (orchestrator→worker snapshot denylist, worker→dispatch runner credential stripping) but the method→child boundary was unprotected.

Key discovery during implementation: Deno's env option on Deno.Command *augments* the parent env rather than replacing it, so clearEnv: true is required to actually prevent leakage.

Test plan

  • [x] Unit tests for createSafeMethodEnv: stripping, case-insensitive matching, allowlist opt-in, empty env edge case
  • [x] Shell model tests: SWAMP_SERVER_TOKEN not in child env, PATH preserved, user env merged correctly
  • [x] All existing

Awarn about --group-refresh-interval only when it is set (swamp-club#2512) (#2624)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-25T01:33:54Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): warn about --group-refresh-interval only when it is set (swamp-club#2512) (#2624)

Summary

swamp serve in a non-OAuth auth mode logged this warning on every boot, even when the operator never set the interval:

--group-refresh-interval is set but --auth-mode oauth is not configured; group refresh is disabled

The guard checked the parsed interval, which falls back to the 4h default, instead of whether a value was supplied.

  • Add shouldWarnGroupRefreshIgnored(raw, intervalMs, oauthReady) in src/cli/commands/serve.ts. It warns only when the raw value from the flag, SWAMP_GROUP_REFRESH_INTERVAL or auth.group-refresh-interval is present, the interval is non-zero, and OAuth group refresh cannot run.
  • oauthReady is the exact negation of the old condition, and the refresh-service construction guard is unchanged, so group refresh behaviour is unaffected.

Fixes swamp-club#2512. Not a regression: the guard has been unchanged since group refresh was introduced in #1861.

Test Plan

  • 4 new unit tests in src/cli/commands/serve_test.ts covering unset, explicit without OAuth, explicit 0, and explicit with OAuth ready. A mutation check confirmed that dropping the raw-defined term fails the unset test.

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T22:57:46Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): refuse resuming a suspended run whose workflow changed shape (swamp-club#2498) (#2623)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2498. swamp workflow resume on a suspended run walks the current workflow against the stored run. Nothing checked that the two still matched, so a workflow edited during the approval window broke only once work had started:

  • Step added to the gate's job. The resume crashed with Step run not found. A step that never ran was left running.
  • Pending step moved to another job. The steps before it ran, a real side effect. Then the resume crashed and left the other job and its step running.
  • Job added. Every step ran and the run was recorded as succeeded. Then the command exited 1 with Job run not found.
  • Pending job removed. The remaining steps ran, and the run ended failed with no failed step.

swamp-club#2433 fixed the same gap for failed runs. This PR covers suspended runs.

Fix

  • Structure check before anything changes. This is the new checkSuspendedRunResume in src/domain/workflows/resume_reset.ts. It shares the job and step rules (a) and (b) with #2433's planFailedRunResume through small module-level helpers, and adds two rules for suspended runs:
  • (c) an unfinished record whose step moved to another job;
  • (d) an unfinished job the workflow no longer has.
  • Removed versus moved. Step names are unique only within a job, so a step counts as moved only when the o

Aend open WebSocket sessions when their token is revoked, rotated or expires (swamp-club#2454) (#2622)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T22:54:17Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): end open WebSocket sessions when their token is revoked, rotated or expires (swamp-club#2454) (#2622)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2454 and folds in swamp-club#2469.

Before this change a server token was checked once, at the WebSocket upgrade. A session then lived until the 8-hour cap, so a revoked, rotated or expired token kept working on every session opened before the change. That held on the instance that revoked it, on HA peers, and after a revoke run from the CLI.

Now each session is bound to the token it was opened with: the name plus the record's createdAt, which rotation rewrites. Sessions end in two ways:

  • Immediately. The access.token.revoke and access.token.rotate handlers close the token's sessions after they reply, even if the request was cancelled after the change was saved. Rotate keeps sessions already opened with the new credential.
  • By revalidation. TokenSessionRevalidationService re-reads the record behind every open session every 30s. It closes the session when the record is:

| Record state | Close code |

|---|---|

| revoked | 4003 |

| rotated | 4003 |

| deleted (ServerTokenNotFoundError) | 4003 |

| unparseable | 4003 |

| expired | 4002 |

This covers revokes run from the CLI and on HA peers, where the record arrives through the runtime data poller. Other read failures, such as I/O, keep the session until the next pass.

Supporting changes:

  • One close path, audited. `terminate
Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/serve/guide.md, design/enablers/remote-execution.md, design/enablers/serve-audit.md

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T22:37:21Zpersistencefix

What's Changed

  • fix(persistence): remove each output's run log in run gc and model delete (swamp-club#2475) (#2621)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2475. swamp run gc deleted model method output records but never their run logs, so logs piled up forever for every model.

gc guessed each log's path by swapping .yaml for .log. But createRunLog names the log from its own new Date() call, a few ms before the output takes its startedAt, so the two names never match (for example …-41-923Z.log next to …-41-927Z.yaml). The guessed file never existed, the NotFound was swallowed, and the real log stayed on disk. It was also left out of the bytes reclaimed, and it kept the method directory from being cleaned up. swamp model delete never removed the log at all. This has been broken since run gc was added, so it is not a regression.

  • Find the log through logFile (src/infrastructure/persistence/yaml_output_repository.ts). An output only owns a log inside its own method directory. That means under the outputs root, or its mirror under the repo's .swamp/outputs/, because run logs are always written there even when outputs live in a datastore.
  • A workflow step's output points at its workflow run's log, which is left for workflow-run gc.
  • A crafted ../ or absolute logFile is ignored.
  • Records without logFile still fall back to the same-name log.
  • swamp model delete removes the output's own log the same way.
  • Datastore sync: logs u
Docs changed: design/surfaces/repo.md

Areap ended step leases and pending dispatches, sync-safely (swamp-club#2262) (#2620)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T22:31:30Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): reap ended step leases and pending dispatches, sync-safely (swamp-club#2262) (#2620)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2262. Step-lease (swamp/step-lease) and pending-dispatch (swamp/pending-dispatch) records declare lifetime: "infinite" and hold one data name per record under a single model instance. Nothing ever deleted them: boot reconciliation only marks stale ones expired/orphaned, and swamp data gc never removes infinite-lifetime data (it only trims version history). Every remote step therefore left a record behind forever, and the boot sweep read all of them on every start.

WorkerGcService now reaps them:

  • Retention rule (src/domain/models/worker/bookkeeping_retention.ts, pure): a record is reapable once it is in a terminal state — lease completed/failed/expired, dispatch dispatched/timed_out/cancelled/orphaned — and its endedAt is at least the grace period (24h) old. Active leases and waiting dispatches are never reapable. The terminal-state sets are now exported from the model files as the single source of truth.
  • Sync-safe reaper (src/serve/bookkeeping_gc.ts): lists candidates through a catalog select projection scoped to the repo's own namespace (reads no bodies), reads each body separately so one unreadable record cannot stall the sweep, and deletes in batches of 100. Each batch deletes and then pushes as one exclusive sync-gate unit, so a poller pull cannot land between a delete and its
Docs changed: design/enablers/datastores.md

Areturn binary file content losslessly from data get (swamp-club#2503) (#2619)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T22:12:24Zdatafix

What's Changed

  • fix(data): return binary file content losslessly from data get (swamp-club#2503) (#2619)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2503. swamp data get (local and --server), serve's data.get, the data search detail view and swamp model output data all decoded artifact bytes with a lenient TextDecoder. Every byte that was not valid UTF-8 became U+FFFD. An image or archive came back as a corrupted string, next to a size and checksum that looked valid, and its bytes could not be recovered.

This change:

  • Adds a content encoder. encodeContent in src/domain/data/content_encoding.ts is a pure function. It decodes with a strict (fatal: true) TextDecoder: valid UTF-8 comes back as text with contentEncoding: "utf-8", exactly as before. Anything else comes back base64-encoded with contentEncoding: "base64", via Uint8Array.prototype.toBase64(), with no new dependency. The choice depends on the bytes, not on the declared content type, so every artifact that round-trips today returns the same string.
  • Uses it in every read path. dataGet (model- and workflow-scoped), data search's detail view and modelOutputData now call it. DataGetData and ModelOutputDataData gain an optional contentEncoding. It reaches serve clients and --server without a protocol change, because both handlers pass the payload through.
  • Parses JSON only from valid UTF-8. model output data encodes before parsing, so an application/json artifact with
Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/data/reference.md, .claude/skills/swamp/references/data/references/fields.md, .claude/skills/swamp/references/data/references/output-shapes.md, .claude/skills/swamp/references/model/references/outputs.md, design/primitives/data.md

2026-09-24T21:42:24Zextensionsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(extensions): delete only skill files an extension owns on rollback, rm and prune (swamp-club#2494) (#2615)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2494. Skills land in shared tool dirs (.claude/skills/<name>, .swamp/pulled-extensions/skills/<name>, ...), so a same-named dir may belong to the user or to another extension. Pull copied into such a dir and recorded only its root, so three delete paths removed the whole dir, including files the extension never wrote:

  • the DuplicateTypeError rollback;
  • extension rm;
  • the upgrade orphan prune.
  • Rollback deletes only what the install created. InstallResult.createdPaths lists a skill root the install created, or only the files it newly wrote into a dir that already existed. rollbackOnCollision deletes createdPaths and uses lstat, so a symlink is unlinked, never followed. The pre-merge snapshot counts symlinks as existing.
  • Lockfile records ownership. A skill is tracked as its root when the extension created the dir or already owned it. A skill merged into a dir it does not own is tracked file by file, so rm and orphan prune delete only those files.
  • Shared claims are kept. rm and the orphan prune keep any path another lockfile entry claims. rm reports these in a new optional retainedFiles field ({ path, claimedBy }), in log and JSON output. The ownership rules live in a new pure domain module, src/domain/extensions/extension_path_claims.ts. They compare paths ignoring separator s

that }} is the Go template's. So swamp model validate blamed {{.Names}} for cutting the expression short and suggested CEL concatenation, when the real fix is the missing }. `fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-24T08:14:29Zauthfix

What's Changed

  • fix(auth): revoke the login key on logout, keep credentials if it can't be revoked (swamp-club#2392) (#2604)

Summary

Fixes the CLI half of swamp-club#2392. swamp auth logout used to delete ~/.config/swamp/auth.json without contacting the server, so the login-issued key stayed valid, and its owner could never revoke a leaked copy.

Logout now revokes the stored key first, through swamp-club's new DELETE /api/v1/me/api-key (a personal key revoking itself; the server takes the key id from the credential). That endpoint is already live. auth.json is deleted only when swamp-club confirms the revoke (2xx with revoked: true) or answers 401 {"error":"Unauthorized"} (the key is already dead). Any other outcome keeps the credentials and exits non-zero:

  • Can be retried (offline, timeout, 5xx, 429, an unconfirmed 2xx, a 401 or 403 that isn't swamp-club's own body): the message says to run logout again.
  • Retry cannot help (404: the server has no endpoint; 403: the credential is not a personal key): the message says to revoke the key in the web UI, then delete the named auth.json.

There is deliberately no flag to skip revoking.

Also in this PR

  • The key only ever goes back to the server that issued it. Logout ignores SWAMP_API_KEY and SWAMP_CLUB_URL.
  • saveIdentityCache no longer re-points a stored login key. Previously, running with SWAMP_API_KEY and a different SWAMP_CLUB_URL rewrote the login file's `serve

2026-09-23T22:53:45Zservefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(serve): add --datastore-poll-interval and cap timer durations (swamp-club#2446) (#2597)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2446.

swamp serve runs three pollers that pull from a remote datastore

(ConfigPoller, AccessDataPoller, RuntimeDataPoller). Each already accepted

pollIntervalMs, but serve never passed it, so peers always converged on a

fixed 30s cycle. One setting now controls all three:

| Surface | Name |

|---|---|

| CLI flag | --datastore-poll-interval (also on serve daemon enable, forwarded by collectServeExtraArgs) |

| Env var | SWAMP_DATASTORE_POLL_INTERVAL |

| serve.yaml key | datastore-poll-interval |

  • Precedence CLI > env > serve.yaml. Unset keeps each poller's 30s default.
  • Minimum 1s: parseTimeout only returns whole seconds. Millisecond input

(500ms) gets its own error naming the flag.

  • With no sync service (filesystem datastore), serve warns the setting has no

effect.

Timer duration limit (all timer-driving flags)

Deno clamps any setTimeout, setInterval or AbortSignal.timeout delay above

2147483647 ms (about 24.8 days) to 1 ms, so an oversized value made interval

loops spin and timeouts abort at once. A shared parseTimerDuration

(src/cli/duration_parser.ts) now rejects such values with

<flag> (<value>) exceeds the maximum safe timer duration (~24.8 days):

  • serve --heartbeat-interval, --reconciliation-interval (limit minus its

500 ms jitter), --group-refresh-interval, `--hydr

Docs changed: design/enablers/access-control.md, design/primitives/serve.md

2026-09-23T22:49:49Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): stop vault, access and extension handlers from forcing full-cache pushes (swamp-club#2415) (#2596)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2415.

Eleven serve paths called syncService.markDirty() with no path before pushChanged. In the S3 and GCS datastore extensions, a bare mark sets bulkInvalidated. The next push then rebuilt the index from every shard, walked and hashed the whole cache, and skipped deletion detection. On MinIO, every vault delete, vault annotate, vault create and access reload did a full push that uploaded nothing. #2459 and #2408 removed the same call from the model, workflow and login-mint paths. #2408 pinned the remaining sites, and this PR removes them.

Each site now signals only what it wrote

| Site | Change |

|---|---|

| access.reload | Relies on the grant repositories' per-path marks. Re-marks the definitions and grant-main folders it wrote, as the mint does. A reload that changes nothing marks nothing. |

| vault.delete, vault.annotate, vault.edit | No push at all, like vault.put. Secrets are always-local, audit entries are repo-local, and edit writes nothing on the server. |

| vault.create, vault.migrate | Mark the vault config file by path (YamlVaultConfigRepository.getPath is now public). Migrate also marks the old path, so the remote copy is deleted rather than restored by the config poller as a second config with the same name. |

| `extension.install

2026-09-23T21:31:56Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): run syncs take the sync gate so a poller pull cannot prune their index entries (swamp-club#2405) (#2588)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2405. On a serve with an S3 datastore, a runtime data poller pull could overlap a workflow run's push, prune the index entries that push had just committed, and CAS-write the pruned shard. The objects stayed in S3, but the index stopped listing them, so data get reported "Data not found".

The sync gate from swamp-club#2247 serialized poller pulls against handler mutations, but every run path pushed outside it: the per-step model-lock flush, and the post-run and post-resume pushes. The fitness test missed this because it only matched literal pushChanged calls, and the step pushes go through acquireModelLocks' flush.

  • Read/write gate. The sync gate is now a FIFO read/write lock (ReadWriteLock, next to Semaphore). Handler mutation units and poller pulls hold it exclusively. Run push units and step-start pulls hold it shared (withSharedSyncGate), so they exclude pulls but keep uploading in parallel with each other.
  • acquireModelLocks gets a wrapSync hook. It wraps only the sync calls, never the model-lock wait. createStepLockHook, executeWorkflowWithLocks and WebhookService take the gate as a required parameter, so the compiler catches a forgotten one, including the scheduler call site outside src/serve.
  • Pollers never make runs wait. A poller takes the gate only when it

check assertStorableServerAddress (src/domain/repo/server_address.ts)

refuses a URL with a userinfo part, a query string or a fragment. It also

refuses a URL that isn't http, https, ws or wss with a host. The check runs

first in RepoService.init, so a refused init leaves the directory untouched

and a refused --force leaves the existing marker as it was. The error shows

the URL only through redactServerUrl, never repeats an unparseable value,

and points to --token, SWAMP_SERVER_TOKEN and swamp auth server-login.

  • auth whoami no longer prints the credential (swamp-club#2449). The

Serve: line and the JSON effectiveServeUrl field printed the effec

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): skip unknown OAuth usernames at startup, add serve check-config (swamp-club#2438, swamp-club#2440) (#2587)

Summary

One unknown username in auth.admins or auth.allowed-users stopped

swamp serve --auth-mode oauth at startup, and under Kubernetes it crash-looped.

Closes swamp-club#2438 and swamp-club#2440.

Startup (swamp-club#2438)

  • A username the provider reports as not found (HTTP 404) is skipped with an

ERROR log, and serve starts with the rest.

  • Any other lookup error (5xx, timeout, network) still aborts startup. Dropping

an existing admin would make materializeAdmins revoke their grant, so a

provider outage must never shrink the list.

  • Serve refuses to start if no admin resolves, or if every allowed-user is

skipped and no collectives are set. checkAdmission admits everyone when both

lists are empty.

  • A skipped name is recorded and stays skipped across restarts and edits to

other entries. It is never looked up again on its own, so someone who

registers a typo'd admin name cannot become an admin. Removing the name drops

its record, and adding it back looks it up fresh.

  • A name that resolved before keeps its cached subject (with a WARN) if the

provider later returns 404, so one bad answer cannot revoke a grant.

The resolution logic moves out of the serve action into

src/serve/oauth_access_list_resolution.ts, with the lookup injected.

resolveUsername throws a typed UsernameNotFoundError on 404.

Astop applying the upgrade chain to unstamped definitions (swamp-club#2412) (#2584)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-23T20:24:04Zmodelsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(models): stop applying the upgrade chain to unstamped definitions (swamp-club#2412) (#2584)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2412.

DefinitionUpgradeService read an absent typeVersion as "predates every upgrade, apply the whole chain". For a definition whose global arguments are already in the shape the current version expects — a hand-written file under models/, which is git-tracked source — that transforms them a second time and corrupts them.

Reproduced against 1da85ec8 before writing the fix: a definition recording fields: [name, path, digest], against a chain whose one upgrade appends digest, comes out as [name, path, digest, digest], and executeWorkflow persists that back to the file.

swamp-club#900 removed the persistence restamp that had been masking this, so the case is now reachable. Absence cannot distinguish the two populations, so it stops being read as a version at all.

The rule

typeVersion gets three readings, deliberately not collapsed into each other:

| Value | Behaviour |

| --- | --- |

| absent | The chain does not run and nothing is stamped. A method run warns when the type declares a chain, so the skip is visible. |

| valid CalVer | Unchanged. |

| anything else | Malformed. UserError, rather than a silent skip. |

Someone who wrote a malformed value stated an intent and got the format wrong; ignoring what they wrote is the same defect as silently corrupting.

The rule now appears at three layers with three

Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/extension/references/model/upgrades.md, design/primitives/models.md

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): carry vault annotate labels as a key-value map over --server (swamp-club#2425) (#2583)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2425. swamp vault annotate <vault> <key> --label k=v --server <url> failed with:

```

invalid_request: payload.labels: expected array, received object

```

The CLI sent labels as a map ({ "team": "infra" }). The vault.annotate wire contract declared labels: string[], and its zod schema enforced that. The server handler then turned each array entry into a label with an empty value, so even a request the schema accepted lost every value (["team=infra"] was stored as "team=infra": ""). Every other layer already uses a map: the VaultAnnotation value object, libswamp's VaultAnnotateInput, and the sibling VaultPutPayload. This contract was the only exception. The mismatch has existed since --server support was added to this command (#1717), so labels over --server never worked.

What changed

  • VaultAnnotatePayload.labels is now Record<string, string> in both src/serve/protocol.ts and packages/client/protocol.ts.
  • VaultAnnotateRequestSchema validates it with z.record(z.string().min(1), z.string()), the same validator vault.put uses. handleVaultAnnotate passes it through unchanged.
  • The CLI's --server payload is pinned with satisfies VaultAnnotatePayload. requestServerResponse types payloads as unknown, which is how this mismatch compiled.
  • Flag parsing and the --clear / no-fie

The version flag is a Cliffy standalone option, answered while arguments are

parsed. Every colour decision lived in .globalAction(), which that path exits

a non-terminal stdout turns colour off on its own.

Three things worth a reviewer's attention:

  • The policy only ever disables. @std/fmt applies NO_COLOR when it loads

and refuses to re-enable afterwards, so calling the switch with true would

override a disable this code did

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): stop the OAuth login mint from forcing a full-cache push (swamp-club#2408) (#2580)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2408. A server login against

a serve with a large remote datastore took up to 90 s, and most of that was the

device-token poll that mints the server token.

mintServerTokenImpl called syncService.markDirty() with no path before

pushChanged. In the S3 and GCS datastore extensions a bare mark sets

bulkInvalidated, so the push ignored the per-path dirty set, rebuilt the index

from every remote shard, and walked and hashed every cached file. All that to

upload the 4 files a login writes. #2459 removed the same call from the model

and workflow handlers.

On a 25k-file serve cache (MinIO, @swamp/s3-datastore), the mint's push drops

from about 5.5 s to about 0.2 s. The login round trip drops from about 6.2 s to

about 0.9 s. A full-walk push forced afterwards finds nothing left to upload.

The race the bare mark was covering

The repositories mark a path dirty *before* they write it. Serve's post-run and

post-resume pushes are ungated, so one can land between the mark and the write.

It finds the path absent, reads that as a delete, and clears the mark. The mint's

push then uploads nothing, and the new token is lost on the next pod restart.

The full walk hid this.

After both writes, the mint now re-marks the token's definition file and its

token-main data folder by path, just before `pushCha

Astop persistence restamping typeVersion, surface staleness (swamp-club#900) (#2577)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-23T17:46:26Zmodelsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(models): stop persistence restamping typeVersion, surface staleness (swamp-club#900) (#2577)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#900, where a model instance appeared to ignore a newly pulled extension bundle.

The schema was never cached — bundleImportUrl content-hashes the bundle, so new code does load. What went stale was the instance's global arguments, and two defects kept it that way:

1. YamlDefinitionRepository.save() overwrote typeVersion on every write. That marked a stale instance as current without migrating its arguments. Because DefinitionUpgradeService short-circuits once typeVersion is at or above the model version, no upgrade chain shipped later could ever run — the instance was stranded permanently. The stamp was incidental to the commit that made definitions self-describing (df04d0dc); the skill docs already state the intended contract, that a version bump needs an upgrade entry even a no-op, which is the mechanism that advances typeVersion.

2. modelGet reported the registered model's version as typeVersion, so an instance whose arguments were never migrated still displayed the new version. That is the value the reporter inspected.

Changes

Creation now owns typeVersion exclusively:

  • save() no longer stamps it, and deliberately does not backfill it either — an absent typeVersion is the recorded signal for a legacy pre-CalVer definition, and backfilling would strand exactly those. Same rule applied to
𝗗 release v20260923.174448.0-sha.c811f8ce𝗖𝗟 c811f8ce𝗣 #2577𝗔 lab#2412

Aredact label-prefixed secrets, stop redacting identifiers (swamp-club#1702) (#2576)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-23T16:39:04Zissuesfix

What's Changed

  • fix(issues): redact label-prefixed secrets, stop redacting identifiers (swamp-club#1702) (#2576)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#1702. A secret shaped

<label>_<payload> escaped redaction entirely unless the label was one of six

enumerated strings, so swamp issue bug posted it verbatim.

\b cannot fire between the joiner and the payload — _ is itself a word

character — so LONG_BASE64_RE and LONG_HEX_RE had no legal place to start

matching. This covers swamp-club's own swamp_<key> format: the redactor's

blind spot included its own credentials, and a real key reached a Lab issue

body before being scrubbed by hand.

The same anchor bug affected five further patterns. card_4111...,

ssn_123-45-6789, host_10.1.2.3 and aws_AKIA... all passed through while

their bare forms redacted. All seven now anchor on a negative lookbehind over

the payload's own character class.

Both directions

Closing the gap generically risks the opposite failure, so the payload rule is

tuned against harvested code rather than intuition. Three tiers of one shape,

differing only in how far the label is trusted:

| tier | label | payload |

| --- | --- | --- |

| enumerated | sk pk rk ak key token secret | must carry key material, or be a UUID |

| known | swamp glpat xoxb xoxp xapp hf shpat dop npm gsk xai | one unbroken run of 20+ alphanumerics |

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-23T16:03:08Zscriptsfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(scripts): fill verify.env from 1Password instead of a heredoc (swamp-club#2411) (#2575)

Summary

The verification workflows need credentials this repository cannot supply, and setting them up was a paragraph of prose and a heredoc. Nothing checked the result — which is how this machine sat with a placeholder in place of TESSL_TOKEN and no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY at all, discovered only by inspecting a run by hand.

deno run setup-verify-env reads one 1Password item per variable, titled by the variable's own name, and writes ~/.config/swamp/verify.env at 0600. --check reports what's missing and writes nothing.

The two keys are not equivalent

| | Missing | Effect |

|---|---|---|

| TESSL_TOKEN | loud | skill-review exits 1, several minutes into a run |

| ANTHROPIC_API_KEY | silent | trigger evals skip at exit 0, reviews fall back to a claude.ai login — the gate goes green having checked less |

The second is the dangerous one, and it is what was happening here. The script reports both, and says which is which.

Three things it deliberately does not do

It names no vault, item path, account or team. This repository is public. A script hardcoding op://SomeTeamVault/... publishes an internal detail as surely as committing a vault config would.

It does not resolve secrets at run time. swamp has @swamp/1password and vault.get(), and a workflow could read these directly — but that meets the unlock prompt *inside a

What's Changed

  • fix(access): opt-in setting so a run grant need not imply approve (swamp-club#2384) (#2571)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2384. A run grant implies approve, so any principal that can run a workflow can also clear its manual_approval gates. An allow grant cannot express "run but not approve", and the only workaround was an explicit deny approve for every group and resource pattern.

The default is unchanged. run still implies approve, so existing grants files keep working exactly as before.

This adds an opt-in serve setting:

  • swamp serve --approve-requires-explicit-grant
  • config key auth.approve-requires-explicit-grant
  • env var SWAMP_APPROVE_REQUIRES_EXPLICIT_GRANT

With it on, an *allow* grant on run no longer satisfies approve, so only a grant that names approve can decide a gate. A *deny* on run still denies approve in both modes, so turning the setting on can only take permissions away.

Changes

  • Domain:
  • GrantBasedAccessDecisionService takes { runImpliesApprove } (default true).
  • AccessDecision.impliedBy: "run" marks a decision that matched approve only through run.
  • actionsCoveredBy(grant) lists which actions a grant covers under the current policy.
  • PolicySnapshotLoader keeps the option across snapshot rebuilds.
  • Serve:
  • Adds the flag, config key and env var, and passes the flag through to the daemon install.
  • Logs the active approval policy at startup, and warn

Resource attributes are stripped from the run record on purpose. That's the fix for swamp-club#1673, where run records ran out of memory. So outputs can't be read back from the record. They now come from:

  • During a run: the step's full output, before it is stripped. No datastore reads.
  • Everywhere else: the datastore, through a new StepOutputResolver domain service (src/domain/workflows/step_output_resolver.ts). That covers resume, a parent reading a child run, and workflow history. The resolver follows the resource references the run record already keeps.

Aretry the racing setup fetch and bind attestations to the executed workflow (swamp-club#2387, swamp-club#2388) (#2566)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-23T00:11:41Zverificationfix

What's Changed

  • fix(verification): retry the racing setup fetch and bind attestations to the executed workflow (swamp-club#2387, swamp-club#2388) (#2566)

Summary

Two fixes to the verification harness. Both touch trust-root paths (verification/**, scripts/build_attestation.ts, agent-constraints/verification-conventions.md), so here's what changed and why.

1. Retry the setup fetch that races under parallel runs (fixes swamp-club#2387)

verify-reviews and verify-skills both run git fetch origin main in their setup step, and the conventions launch them together. Every worktree shares refs/remotes/origin/main. When main has moved since the last fetch, both fetches try to advance the ref from the same old value. The loser fails with incorrect old value provided, set -e fails setup, and the run reports what looks like a skills or review failure.

Both setup steps now run:

```sh

git fetch origin main || { sleep 1; git fetch origin main; }

```

By the time the retry runs, the ref is already current, so it has nothing to write. The one-second pause covers a winner that still holds the ref lock. A fetch that fails twice still fails the step. The two setup steps stay identical in shape, and verify-build is unchanged because it doesn't fetch.

2. Refuse attestations for runs of a different workflow (fixes swamp-club#2388)

build_attestation.ts pinned each workflow's hash at the verified commit, but the commit is only an input the run was handed. W

Astop server-login waiting on the browser it launched (swamp-club#2378) (#2563)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T23:36:06Zauthfix

What's Changed

  • fix(auth): stop server-login waiting on the browser it launched (swamp-club#2378) (#2563)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2378. swamp auth server-login hung forever after the user approved the code in the browser. It printed Opening browser... and nothing else, and it never timed out.

Root cause. openBrowser awaited Deno.Command.output(), which waits for the launcher to exit *and* for its stdout/stderr pipes to close. On sway, xdg-open's generic path runs the default browser in the foreground when it isn't already open, so xdg-open doesn't return until the browser quits. serverLogin waited for that before its polling loop, and the 300 s timeout only covered the fetches, so nothing ended the wait. When the browser was already running, the same command finished in 0.76 s.

Changes

  • openBrowser (src/infrastructure/process/browser.ts): spawns the launcher with null stdio, so no browser process can hold a pipe to swamp open. It waits for the launcher's exit for up to BROWSER_LAUNCH_GRACE_MS (2 s):
  • exits 0: success;
  • exits non-zero, or fails to spawn: the existing "Could not open a browser" UserError;
  • still running at 2 s: treated as launched, unref()'d and not awaited.

The timer is cleared in a finally. Spawning is injectable, so the tests use fakes. This also fixes swamp auth login, the mailto handoff in issue submit, and the extension report dispatcher, which share the helper.

  • serverLogin (`src/li

Agenerate the attestation, and give it a schema (swamp-club#2376) (#2560)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T21:56:47Zverificationfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(verification): generate the attestation, and give it a schema (swamp-club#2376) (#2560)

Summary

The attestation's shape was real long before anything enforced it. .github/workflows/ci.yml reads about two dozen specific paths out of it with jq, and verification-conventions.md spent seventy lines telling an agent how to produce each one. Between those two, nothing checked anything: post_attestation took a string, JSON.parsed it, posted whatever came back, and then read (parsed.subject as Record<string, unknown>)?.commit with an unchecked cast.

AttestationSchema is that shape written down once. The generator produces documents satisfying it and post_attestation refuses ones that don't, so the contract is enforced where the document is made and again where it leaves the machine — rather than in bash on the far side, after the PR is public.

Demonstrated on this branch, posting a document with subject removed:

```

Error: attestation does not match AttestationSchema:

subject: Invalid input: expected object, received undefined

```

On main that same document posts cleanly and logs commit=undefined.

The generator

scripts/build_attestation.ts takes the three verify-* run ids in any order and reads each record's own workflowName to tell them apart, so mislabelling one is not a mistake that can be made. buildAttestation() is pure — every field is a function of the run records, the workflow definitions at the verified

Apersist why a step was skipped (swamp-club#2358) (#2558)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T21:23:26Zworkflowsfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(workflows): persist why a step was skipped (swamp-club#2358) (#2558)

Summary

A skipped StepRun recorded only its status, so a step a path guard correctly excluded and one whose job never started were the same fact downstream: a bare skipped, counted by CI. The execution service already knew the difference — it yields step_skipped with a reason and, for a guard, the expression — but nothing persisted it, so the distinction died with the event stream.

This matters most in the verification attestation. reviewConfig is meant to say, per review, whether it ran and why not; today an agent types that by hand because the run record cannot answer it.

What changed

StepRun.skip now takes a StepSkipReason (dependency, guarded with the evaluated expression, or job_skipped for the cascade from JobRun.skip), persisted through toData/fromData and cleared on resetToPending. The run view exposes it, workflow history get prints it in log mode, and the verification report surfaces both a per-step reason and a gate.skippedByKind breakdown.

A skip with no recorded reason reads as reason not recorded rather than a bare skipped. Runs persisted before this field are legitimately silent, and saying so beats inviting the reader to assume a guard.

The reason is a discriminated union, not one object with an optional expression. Only a guard has an expression to report, and a flat shape validates `{ kind: "dependency", expres

Summary

The output was already in memory; it went out of scope on the way to the throw. Not a race — a statement ordering, so every failing shell step in every workflow lost its output, every time.

The cost lands on exactly the steps that matter. A review step in verify-reviews exits 1 in two very different situations: the claude CLI died, or check_review_verdict.ts returned a genuine VERDICT: fail. One is noise to retry; the other is the gate correctly refusing the change and must never be retried away. With no log, both arrive as Command exited with code 1 and nothing else, and the attestation records status: failed for both with no evidence either way.

That is not hypothetical. Three verification runs this week each had one failed review that could not be classified without re-running it by hand outside the harness:

| 7d11c1cf | adversarial-review | none (4 other steps logged) |

| 007fc135 | run-tests | none (6 other steps logged) |

| 193b1ff1 | code-review | none (2 other steps logged) |

A failed run-tests is the same story with no failing test named.

2026-09-22T20:38:33Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): accept resume --from and other stripped request fields (swamp-club#2356) (#2554)

Summary

swamp workflow resume --from <step> sent to a serve refused every failed run with Run <id> is not suspended (status: failed) (swamp-club#2356).

The serve checks each WebSocket request against a zod schema before dispatch, and zod silently drops any field the schema doesn't list. WorkflowResumeRequestSchema never listed from, so the handler never saw it and fell back to the suspended-run resolver. --from was added in #1998 without touching this schema, so it has never worked through serve. The local path was unaffected.

An audit of every request type found the same drift dropping three more CLI flags over serve:

| Request | Field | CLI flag |

|---|---|---|

| workflow.resume | from | workflow resume --from |

| workflow.run | noSupersede | workflow run --no-supersede |

| model.search | includeInternal | model search --all |

| vault.audit-trail | action | vault audit-trail --action |

Astop a loader test depending on where Deno is installed (swamp-club#2350) (#2552)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T20:10:04Zextensionsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(extensions): stop a loader test depending on where Deno is installed (swamp-club#2350) (#2552)

Summary

load: indexOnly records bundling failures asserts that bundling a source file with no pre-existing bundle fails. It fails by *not finding the Deno binary* — and the stub runtime shared by every test in the file named that binary /usr/bin/deno, which is exactly where a distro package puts a perfectly real one.

So on any machine that installed Deno from its package manager, the bundle succeeded, result.failed came back empty, and the assertion blew up:

```

AssertionError: Values are not equal: bundling failure should be recorded

  • false

+ true

```

On a machine where Deno lives anywhere else — a container image, ~/.deno, asdf, mise — the path is absent and the test passes. That is why this read as an intermittent failure belonging to whoever happened to hit it, rather than as a wrong constant: it is perfectly deterministic per machine.

The consequence is not just a noisy suite. run-tests is a gating step of the verify-build workflow, so on an affected machine the build gate cannot go green, compile and binary-check are skipped by dependency, and no branch can carry a valid attestation — the test blocks every PR from that machine regardless of what the branch changed.

The stub now names a path no machine has. Nothing in the file wants a working Deno: the tests that bundle successfully take the trustPulledCache fast path,

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T18:58:57Zworkerfix

What's Changed

  • fix(worker): apply --ca-cert to the dispatch data plane (swamp-club#2193) (#2551)

Summary

swamp worker connect --ca-cert / SWAMP_CA_CERT only reached the worker's control socket. Each dispatch runner built its DataPlaneClient on the global fetch, so against an orchestrator with a private CA the worker enrolled and then every dispatch failed with fetch failed unless DENO_CERT was set.

  • Carry the CA to the runner. RunnerBootstrapParams gains optional caCerts (PEM contents). runWorker passes its certificates to the dispatch handler, and the new buildRunnerBootstrapParams puts them in the bootstrap frame. They travel in that frame rather than the spawn environment, which the orchestrator's environment snapshot can overlay.
  • Trust it on every data-plane request. New createDataPlaneFetch binds all of the runner's data-plane requests to one Deno.HttpClient built with those certificates via the existing createTlsHttpClient. The runner now builds its client inside the existing try, so a TLS client failure fails the dispatch instead of crashing the runner.
  • Design doc. design/enablers/remote-execution.md now says --ca-cert covers both planes and lists the new bootstrap field.
  • Unrelated test fix. In extension_loader_test.ts, the stub deno path moves from /usr/bin/deno to a path that never exists. On hosts with a distro-packaged deno there, the "indexOnly records bundling failures" test spawned a real den
𝗗 release v20260922.185723.0-sha.33229b53𝗖𝗟 33229b53𝗣 #2551𝗔 lab#2193

Acontent-address extension bundle imports so hot-reload stops leaking heap (swamp-club#2340) (#2549)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T18:38:51Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): content-address extension bundle imports so hot-reload stops leaking heap (swamp-club#2340) (#2549)

Summary

Every swamp serve hot-reload (SIGHUP, or the config poller seeing changed pulled-extension files) bumped a process-global reload generation and re-imported every pulled extension bundle under a fresh ?gen=N URL. The ES module registry keys modules by URL and never evicts them, so each reload stacked a full copy of every extension on the heap until V8 hit its heap limit.

  • Import URLs are now content-addressed: ?fp=<source fingerprint>&h=<sha256 of bundle bytes> via a new bundleImportUrl helper in src/domain/extensions/extension_loader.ts, used by both importBundleByPath and importBundle.
  • Unchanged bundles map to the same URL and reuse their cached module. A re-bundled extension gets a new URL, so changed code still runs after reload (keeps the swamp-club#1140 fix).
  • Removed reloadGeneration / incrementReloadGeneration and its libswamp re-export. Its only consumer was reloadPulledExtensions.
  • Updated design/primitives/serve.md and design/enablers/remote-execution.md. The remaining limitation: V8 can't evict ES modules, so the heap grows once per genuinely changed bundle, not once per reload.

Hashing costs about 1.7 ms per bundle (187 ms for all 112 @swamp/aws/ec2 bundles, 118 MB). That is well below the V8 parse and compile an unchanged bundle no longer pays on each reload.

  • Unit te

Adocument memory contract for pullChanged and add heap diagnostics (swamp-club#2328) (#2536)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T23:25:30Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): document memory contract for pullChanged and add heap diagnostics (swamp-club#2328) (#2536)

Summary

  • Document the memory contract for DatastoreSyncService.pullChanged: implementations must not retain file content buffers after writing to the local cache, must be aware of the serve process-lifetime singleton pattern, and should bound internal indexes.
  • Add periodic V8 heap stats debug logging to RuntimeDataPoller so operators can spot heap growth without full OTel instrumentation.
  • Add memory contract documentation to the conformance suite in packages/testing/.

The root cause of the heap growth is in @swamp/s3-datastore (file bodies buffered as Uint8Array via transformToByteArray instead of streamed to disk, and local file hash comparison via Deno.readFile instead of streaming hashers). That fix is being handled in the extensions repo. This PR documents the expectations in core so future extensions don't repeat the pattern.

Aexplain a data.latest() lookup that found no record (swamp-club#2311) (#2534)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T22:43:58Zcelfix

What's Changed

  • fix(cel): explain a data.latest() lookup that found no record (swamp-club#2311) (#2534)

Summary

Closes swamp-club#2311.

data.latest() and data.version() return null when no record matches, and a

plain . select off that null raises cel-js's generic No such key: attributes

with the caret under the field. The caret blames the traversal and hides the real

cause — the record is missing. attributes is always present on a DataRecord,

so the field was never the problem.

The issue reported this as an asymmetry between workflow assert steps and

model_method inputs. That asymmetry does not exist — both paths construct

the same CelEvaluator and fail identically. Reproduced in a scratch repo:

| Expression | assert step | model_method input |

| --- | --- | --- |

| data.latest("echo-hi","result").attributes.exitCode == 0 | passes | — |

| ...attributes.stdout.split(" ").all(w, w.size() > 0) | passes | — |

| data.latest("echo-hi","nope").attributes.exitCode | No such key: attributes | No such key: attributes |

What changed

CelDataNamespace records lookups that resolved to no record, threading the note

through the promise so it lands before cel-js selects a field off the result.

CelEvaluator reads that back in its catch and appends an explanation below

cel-js's own snippet, so the caret survives:

```

Invalid expression: No such key: attributes

> 1 | data.latest("echo-hi", "nope").attributes.exitCode == 0

Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/data/references/troubleshooting.md

Areport the run outcome in the run tracker (swamp-club#2308) (#2531)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T21:08:17Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): report the run outcome in the run tracker (swamp-club#2308) (#2531)

Summary

swamp run history reported completed for workflow runs that actually failed, contradicting swamp workflow history search for the same run id.

The run tracker was marked completed on the normal exit path *before* run.complete() derived the outcome from the step results, so a run whose steps failed still wrote completed. The tracker meant "the process finished", while the field it exposes is named status. The resume path repeated the same pattern.

Both completion paths now call run.complete() first and map the aggregate status through trackerStatusForRun() — an exhaustive switch over all seven WorkflowRun statuses with no default, so a status added later fails the type check rather than being read as success.

Not a regression: the line has read "completed" since the run tracker landed in fe08c4dc.

Test Plan

  • Unit tests in execution_service_test.ts: a failing workflow records failed, a succeeding one still records completed, a resume that fails after approval records suspended then failed, and a table test covers all seven mapping arms.
  • Reproduced and verified end to end in a scratch repo against the compiled binary. For a one-step workflow exiting 3, both commands now agree on failed; a passing workflow still reports completed.
  • Verification workflow green at ba379c49: build 9/9, code-review and adversarial-r

Areconcile the installed version against the lockfile pin (swamp-club#2150) (#2529)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T20:55:33Zextensionsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(extensions): reconcile the installed version against the lockfile pin (swamp-club#2150) (#2529)

Summary

swamp extension install decided an entry was satisfied from file presence, layout generation, and — only when the entry carried one — the recorded filesChecksum. A different version's files already sitting in the per-extension subtree, e.g. left by doctor extensions resolving a missing type, therefore passed as up_to_date on any entry written before filesChecksum tracking. The pin never took effect and nothing said so.

Install now compares the pinned version against the version in the installed manifest.yaml before the content check. A constraint pin (>=, ^, ~) and a missing or malformed manifest yield no comparison, so neither re-pulls on every run. The lockfile-supplied entry name is validated with assertContainedPath before either check touches the disk.

doctor extensions --repair re-pulled with version: null, resolving registry latest and rewriting the entry — the other way a pin was lost. It now restores the pinned version; extensions with no lockfile entry still resolve latest. Behaviour change: a pinned version that is yanked or unavailable now fails the repair operation instead of silently recovering to latest. The lockfile is the intended authority, so that ordering is deliberate.

Also documents the reconciliation rule in design/primitives/extensions.md, next to the existing integrity-anchor contract.

| Defect | Symptom |

| --- | --- |

terminal size from ambient state, so behaviour depended on where the suite ran.

Root cause of the reported failure

Deno.env (verified directly: two files, same pid, each observing the other's

env writes). RepoService.init/.upgrade resolved their user-level write

targets from that environment, so a repo init in one file installed bundled

skills into whatever HOME another file had momentarily assigned.

mid-teardown and its recursive delete failed with

Abuild HTTP clients lazily and name the CA-store remedy (swamp-club#2293) (#2523)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T15:57:17Zclifix

What's Changed

  • fix(cli): build HTTP clients lazily and name the CA-store remedy (swamp-club#2293) (#2523)

Summary

Deno.createHttpClient() reads the platform certificate store, and both

src/cli/remote_run.ts and src/worker/connect.ts built one at module scope.

remote_run.ts sits on the CLI's import path, so every invocation — including

offline ones like swamp help and swamp model search — forced that read before

argument parsing. On a macOS host where the read fails, the process died with an

uncaught Failed to load platform certificates: SecTrustSettingsCopyCertificates,

which also blocked the built-in issue-reporting command.

Both module-scope clients are gone. Construction happens on first use, behind

createTlsHttpClient(), which converts an unreadable store into a UserError

naming DENO_TLS_CA_STORE=mozilla — the override the reporter found. Every

construction site routes through it, including the CA-certificate paths in

createSocket(), runWorker() and auth server-login: Deno documents caCerts

as used *in addition to* the default roots, so --ca-cert supplements the failing

store read rather than replacing it, and the message offers it only for a private

root.

What this does not do: commands that actually reach the network still fail on

a host where the store is unreadable. They now fail with guidance naming the

working override instead of an uncaught error. Local commands work regardless,

which is what the issue asks for.

Areport webhook run outcomes honestly (swamp-club#2303) (#2519)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-21T15:36:38Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): report webhook run outcomes honestly (swamp-club#2303) (#2519)

Summary

WebhookService captured only the started and completed events from the workflow run stream, so any run that ended another way fell into the success branch: it was logged as completed with an empty run id and emitted webhook_completed, which feeds the completed bucket of the health endpoint's throughput metrics. Failed webhook runs were inflating health throughput and hiding themselves from operators.

The scheduled sibling fixed the error-event case in ffcdee12 for swamp-club#693; the webhook path never received the same treatment.

This change captures the error, cancelled and suspended events and requires an explicit succeeded status for success. A cancelled run reports the cancellation, an errored run reports the stream error, and a run suspended on an approval gate logs at info and emits no terminal event — it has not finished, so neither terminal event would be true, and it contributes no health record until it resumes.

Scope notes

  • The reporter's exact scenario, an expression failure in trigger.inputs, already logged a failure correctly: it throws out of executeWorkflowWithLocks before this branch is reached. | Function | On failure | Used by |

| --- | --- | --- |

| recordLifecycle | raises | every mandatory audit entry |

| recordLifecycleBestEffort | warns | the assignment entry in start |

| recordUpstreamChange | raises | status transitions and type updates |

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-18T15:42:32Zclifix

What's Changed

  • fix(cli): write interactive prompts to stderr (swamp-club#2260) (#2512)

Summary

src/cli/prompt_helpers.ts wrote every prompt to stdout. For most commands that is untidy at worst; for a command whose stdout carries a value it is corruption:

```bash

swamp vault read-secret myvault mykey > key.pem

# key.pem: "This will reveal the secret 'mykey' from vault 'reprovault'. Continue? [y/N] SECRET-VALUE-123"

```

The byte-exact stdout that swamp-club#1768 established is lost to a prompt. The existing assertInteractiveStdin() gate does not help — redirecting *stdout* does not change *stdin*, so the prompt still fires and still lands in the redirect target. That is also why no test caught it: the failure needs a real TTY on stdin and a redirected stdout at the same time.

Prompts are interaction, not output. All four writes in promptLine and promptChoice now go to stderr; promptConfirmation and promptLineWithDefault inherit the fix through promptLine. Every prompting command is covered at once, with no dependency on the value-on-stdout registry.

This is the second half of the vault read-secret stdout contract — swamp-club#2254 (merged, #2507) fixed LogTape records reaching stdout; prompts do not go through LogTape, so stderrOnly had no reach over them.

  • src/cli/prompt_helpers.ts — four Deno.stdout.write → Deno.stderr.write; module doc states the rule and why the stdin gate does not cover it.
  • **`src/cli/promp

Agate poller pulls against handler mutations (swamp-club#2247) (#2504)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T21:34:57Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): gate poller pulls against handler mutations (swamp-club#2247) (#2504)

Summary

A delete over --server could come back. A handler delete is two steps — remove

the file from the local cache, then pushChanged() — and the push decides

delete-vs-upload by looking at the file on disk (markDirty rule 2,

"absence-on-disk = delete"). A poller pull that downloaded the item between

those two steps put it back on disk, so the push re-uploaded it instead of

deleting it, and the delete was silently undone.

This was filed as a known follow-up of swamp-club#2240, which added the pushes

that made the window observable.

  • New src/serve/sync_gate.ts: one in-process permit (reusing the existing

Semaphore) held across a whole mutation+push unit and across each poller

pull, so the two can never interleave.

  • Acquired in exactly three places: the 28 mutating dispatch cases in

connection.ts (derived from the actual pushChanged call sites, including

the four vault verbs), the server-token mint in device_auth_handler.ts

(serve's only non-WebSocket push path), and each poller's pullChanged.

  • Poller pulls are now hard-bounded via the existing runBoundedSync — they

previously passed no AbortSignal at all, so a hung extension pull would

have held the gate forever. The gate wait is bounded above the pull bound and

degrades to ungated, so a wedged holder cannot stall the server while a

merely slow pull never disables the fix.

Areap orphaned YAML workflow runs after rolling restarts (#2498)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T19:10:24Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): reap orphaned YAML workflow runs after rolling restarts (#2498)

Summary

Fixes two gaps in the HA reconciliation pipeline that left YAML workflow-run

records stuck in running status permanently after rolling restarts

(swamp-club#2243, reported by @bixu).

  • Boot reap heartbeat lookup: reapOrphanedWorkflowRuns() now accepts an

optional heartbeatLookup callback. When a run belongs to a foreign instance

not in the local tracker, and a remote control plane is available, it checks

whether that instance's heartbeat still exists. No heartbeat → reap the run.

Without the lookup (filesystem-only deployments), the existing skip-foreign

behavior is preserved.

  • Continuous reconciler YAML update: reconcileRemoteInterruptedRuns() now

accepts an optional workflowRunRepo dep. After marking tracker entries as

interrupted for stale instances, it also loads and interrupts their YAML

workflow-run records.

  • Doctor safety net: run doctor --fix --server now detects and reaps

orphaned YAML workflow runs whose owning instance has no heartbeat, giving

operators an escape hatch beyond automatic reconciliation.

Test plan

  • [x] 3 new unit tests for reapOrphanedWorkflowRuns heartbeat lookup

(no heartbeat → reap, live heartbeat → skip, no lookup → skip)

  • [x] 3 new unit tests for reconcileRemoteInterruptedRuns YAML reconciliation

(stale instance YAML reaped, alive instance skipped, no repo → existing behavior)

https://swamp.club/r/F7WHBGQCPN

{ "code": "F7WHBGQCPN", "url": "https://swamp.club/r/F7WHBGQCPN" }

```

Closes swamp-club#2180. Server side is swamp-club#2179 (merged and deployed).

Design notes

The stdout/stderr split is the point. Log mode puts the bare, uncoloured URL alone on stdout and every word of prose on stderr, so the link pipes cleanly. The renderer tests assert both streams separately, because this is exactly the kind of thing a later refactor collapses by accident.

first-rule is an easter egg. It's the same command under a second name, registered .hidden() — absent from help, shell completions and the swamp help --json schema, but reachable when typed. It's a hidden *sibling* rather than an alias because Cliffy prints aliases in the group listing (link, first-rule), and an easter egg listed in help isn't one. Both names come from one factory, so they can't drift.

Pure group, no positional. An earlier revision carried an optional [email] positional standing in for the

Adiff the verified worktree, not the caller's checkout (#2482)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T00:42:54Zverificationfix

What's Changed

  • fix(verification): diff the verified worktree, not the caller's checkout (#2482)

Summary

The detect-changes jobs in verify-reviews and verify-skills call @swamp/git without repoPath. It defaults to ".", so the diff ran in whatever checkout swamp workflow run was invoked from — not the worktree created at the verified commit. That checkout is normally clean and sitting on origin/main, so the diff came back empty, every path guard's .size() == 0 was trivially true, and every guarded step skipped while the workflow reported success.

This fixes both workflows and documents the trap.

Fixes swamp-club#2226.

What was actually happening

Observed on a branch touching src/cli/, src/infrastructure/, src/libswamp/ and src/presentation/ — run 9df7e3e0:

```

code-review succeeded

adversarial-review skipped ← should have run

ux-review skipped ← should have run

ci-security-review skipped ← correct, no .github/workflows/ changes

```

Only code-review ran, because it is unguarded and its shell step sets workingDir to the verified worktree, where it computes its own merge-base diff. The guards and the review were reading different repositories:

```

main checkout (where the diff steps ran):

HEAD 93897fc2 == origin/main → git diff --name-only origin/main → 0 files

verified worktree (where the reviews ran):

HEAD 2320995d → merge-base diff → 13 files

```

Ause clock-based expiry in mint guard (#2486)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T00:16:46Ztokensfix

What's Changed

  • fix(tokens): use clock-based expiry in mint guard (#2486)

Summary

  • Enrollment and server token mint() guards checked only stored state when

deciding whether to allow re-minting. The expire() transition only fires for

connected workers, so tokens that lapsed without a connected worker kept their

stored state as unused/enrolled/active forever, blocking re-minting.

  • Now both mint guards also check isExpired(parsed, Date.now()), treating

tokens past their expiresAt as effectively expired regardless of stored state.

This matches the derived-state pattern already used by redeem()/authenticate()

and effectiveTokenState() in the presentation layer.

  • Added three tests covering re-mint after clock expiry without explicit expire():

unused enrollment token, enrolled enrollment token, and active server token.

Closes swamp-club#2191

Aresolve repo root from inside a linked git worktree (swamp-club#2181) (#2477)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T21:52:09Zclifix

What's Changed

  • fix(cli): resolve repo root from inside a linked git worktree (swamp-club#2181) (#2477)

Summary

findAncestorRepoDir walks up from the start directory looking for the

.swamp.yaml marker and stops at the git root. In a linked worktree the git

root is the worktree, so traversal stopped before reaching the main working

tree where the marker lives. Every swamp command failed from a worktree with

"Not a swamp repository" unless --repo-dir was passed on each subcommand.

This widens the stop boundary to the main working tree when started from a

linked worktree, derived from git rev-parse --git-common-dir. Guards preserve

today's behavior everywhere else: the common dir must be named .git, the

derived main working tree must be at or above the git root, and it must exist.

Plain checkouts, bare repositories, unusual GIT_DIR layouts, and worktrees

parked outside the main checkout all fall back unchanged.

Note that --git-common-dir is relative to git's cwd in the main working tree

but absolute from inside a linked worktree, so the result is resolved against

the start directory rather than used as-is. Using --path-format=absolute

would also work but imposes a git ≥2.31 floor for no benefit.

Test changes

Five existing assertions compared the resolved repo dir to Deno.cwd(). They

passed only because the suite happened to run from the repo root; once

resolution works from a worktree the cwd is no longer the answer. They now

assert the

𝗗 release v20260916.215025.0-sha.93897fc2𝗖𝗟 93897fc2𝗣 #2477𝗔 lab#2181

Aassert cert parsing explicitly instead of openssl defaults (swamp-club#2199) (#2475)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T21:43:59Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): assert cert parsing explicitly instead of openssl defaults (swamp-club#2199) (#2475)

Summary

Four TLS tests shelled out to openssl and relied on OpenSSL 3.x defaults.

macOS ships LibreSSL as /usr/bin/openssl, where req -x509 emits a v1

certificate carrying no extensions at all, so:

  • hasCaTrue returned false where CA:TRUE was expected
  • validateEndEntityCert produced no ca-true warning
  • the private-CA chain was rejected at handshake as UnsupportedCertVersion

rather than reaching the expected UnknownIssuer, because a v1 CA cert

cannot be parsed as part of the chain

deno run test therefore exited 1 on any macOS host using the system openssl,

which blocked the run-tests step of verify-build and with it every

attestation produced from such a host.

This passes basicConstraints explicitly at generation instead of inheriting

whatever the local openssl happens to default to, so the assertions exercise

our own cert parsing rather than the tool's defaults. The CA in the trust

integration test also gets an explicit keyUsage, as befits a CA.

The CA:FALSE cases already passed -addext and were unaffected, which

confirms LibreSSL supports the flag.

Closes swamp-club#2199

Test Plan

  • [x] deno run test src/infrastructure/runtime/tls_cert_validation_test.ts — 6 passed, 0 failed

Adefer query body reads until evaluation needs them (swamp-club#2122) (#2469)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T19:13:31Zdatafix

What's Changed

  • fix(data): defer query body reads until evaluation needs them (swamp-club#2122) (#2469)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2122. DataQueryService.executeQuery() read every candidate row's body before evaluating the CEL predicate. SQL pushdown only covers is_latest and model_name, so a query like specName == "question" && attributes.value > 0 read the body of every unrelated row. A single unreadable unrelated body aborted the whole query with Permission denied.

  • Each row is evaluated against a metadata-only record. attributes/content are memoizing getters on the CEL context, so a body loads only when evaluation touches it or a matched row is materialized.
  • Returned records stay plain DataRecords, so foreign-content hydration, vault resolution and JSON output are unchanged.
  • Required read failures still fail the query. That includes reads that CEL error absorption swallows (<error> || true); they resurface when the matched row is materialized. Ordinary CEL evaluation errors (for example a missing key) keep the existing skip behavior.
  • The stale-row filter (swamp-club#1737), the order in which the limit applies before bodies load, and the JSON content/attributes alias are unchanged.
  • design/enablers/data-query.md now describes per-row, access-driven loading.

Known limit: operand order matters. With attributes.value > 0 && specName == "question", bodies are still read, but an unreadable unrelated body no longer aborts the query. Re

𝗗 release v20260916.191155.0-sha.c30806e5𝗖𝗟 c30806e5𝗣 #2469𝗔 lab#2122

Arecover interrupted runs from durable checkpoints (swamp-club#2153) (#2472)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T18:56:03Zworkflowsfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(workflows): recover interrupted runs from durable checkpoints (swamp-club#2153) (#2472)

Summary

  • Add unknown step status and interrupted run status to the domain model — when serve crashes, in-flight steps are marked unknown (outcome ambiguous) and runs are marked interrupted (recoverable), replacing the previous behavior of marking everything as failed
  • Save per-step checkpoints during execution (after each step completes, not just per topological level) so a mid-level crash preserves completed steps
  • Persist run plan identity at run start — fingerprint the evaluated workflow and store a per-run snapshot for recovery comparison
  • Add swamp workflow recover CLI command that assesses recovery eligibility (guard-based auto-recovery vs operator acknowledgement) and resets unknown steps for re-execution via swamp workflow resume
  • Update run tracker and HA boot reconciliation to use interrupted status instead of failed for reaped runs
  • Update design docs with recovery semantics

Recovery model

Recovery requires all unknown steps to have guard expressions for auto-recovery, or explicit --acknowledge-unknown for unguarded steps. Fingerprint checking detects workflow definition drift and refuses recovery when the definition changed. This does not promise exactly-once execution — the unknown status makes crash ambiguity visible.

Test plan

  • [x] 115 workflow run unit tests (8 new for unknown/interrupted/recovery)

What's Changed

  • fix(data): let data.latest() see ephemeral data in the light expression context (swamp-club#2188) (#2471)

data.latest() falls back to DataQueryService.getLatestRecord() when the model isn't in the resolver's coordinates map. The light expression context that #2468 introduced has an empty map, so every lookup takes that fallback. Models created during a run already hit the same gap before #2468.

  • libswamp/workflows/run.ts: creates the ephemeral store in the data repo's namespace, as resume, serve and model run already do. Without this, the lookup still missed ephemeral rows in repos with a datastore namespace set.

Aresolve full run UUIDs without loading global run history (swamp-club#2125) (#2465)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-15T23:23:45Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): resolve full run UUIDs without loading global run history (swamp-club#2125) (#2465)

Summary

swamp workflow history get <full-run-uuid> and swamp workflow history logs <full-run-uuid> routed every complete UUID through matchRunByPartialId(), which called YamlWorkflowRunRepository.findAllGlobal() before comparing the ID. That fans out over every workflow directory, hydrates every retained run aggregate, and sorts them all — so inspecting one known run cost a read and parse of the entire run history.

Measured with an instrumented reproduction (5 workflows × 20 runs, Deno.readTextFile wrapped, one complete UUID through both real deps factories):

| | run files read | target reads |

| --- | --- | --- |

| before | 100 of 100 | 1 |

| after | 1 | 1 |

Changes

  • YamlWorkflowRunRepository.findGlobalById() — locates the owning workflow by Deno.stat'ing the deterministic path save() writes (<baseDir>/<workflowId>/workflow-run-<runId>.yaml), then delegates to the existing findById() only on a hit. One stat per workflow directory, no directory enumeration, exactly one YAML parse. A null from findById after a successful stat — the concurrent-delete race with GC / deleteOlderThan / deleteAllByWorkflowId — continues the scan rather than abandoning it, matching the NotFound-means-skip convention already documented in findAllByWorkflowId. Kept off the WorkflowRunRepository port following the precedent `fi
𝗗 release v20260915.232201.0-sha.6de30270𝗖𝗟 6de30270𝗣 #2465𝗔 lab#2125

Ashorten initial retry interval for per-model locks (swamp-club#2126) (#2464)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-15T22:38:21Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): shorten initial retry interval for per-model locks (swamp-club#2126) (#2464)

Fixes swamp-club#2126.

Problem

createModelLock() built its FileLock with only lockKey and maxWaitMs, so retryIntervalMs fell back to DEFAULT_RETRY_INTERVAL_MS (1s). A waiter that hit a live holder therefore slept ~750–1250 ms (1s ± 25% jitter) before retrying, even when the holder released milliseconds later — avoidable latency on brief local lock handoffs, including resumed workflow steps.

Change

Per-model locks now start their backoff at 25 ms (MODEL_LOCK_RETRY_INTERVAL_MS). Backoff still doubles from there and caps at 8 s, so sustained contention converges on the previous cadence. Jitter, timeout, ownership, heartbeat and stale-lock handling are unchanged.

Deliberately unchanged: FileLock's 1 s default (global datastore lock, breakglass lock, auto-definition lock) and custom datastore provider lock policy, which comes from each provider's own createLock().

FileLock.retryIntervalMs goes from private readonly to readonly so the test can assert the configured policy — AGENTS.md forbids wall-clock duration assertions, so the test targets configuration rather than measured latency.

Verification

Two contenders on one lock key, holder releasing at 50 ms:

| | waiter acquired |

| --- | --- |

| before | 1076 ms |

| after | 84 ms |

Local verification workflows on 3180434f: build ✓ (lint, fmt, type-check, 66 s test run, deps au

Aremove redundant bare markDirty() from serve handlers to fix deletion propagation (swamp-club#2154) (#2459)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-15T19:07:36Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): remove redundant bare markDirty() from serve handlers to fix deletion propagation (swamp-club#2154) (#2459)

Summary

  • Remove redundant bare markDirty() calls from serve mutation handlers

where repositories already send per-path markDirty({ relPath }) signals

  • Update model handler sync test to verify per-path signals flow through

and no bare override occurs

  • Document the "serve handler obligation" in design/enablers/datastores.md

Fixes swamp-club#2154 — model delete via --server never propagated the

deletion to the remote S3 datastore.

Root cause: The serve handler's bare markDirty() (added in 661f4896

for managedConfig, before repos had per-path signals) sets

bulkInvalidated in the S3 extension, forcing a full-walk pushChanged

path that skips deletion detection. PR #2393 later wired per-path

markDirty({ relPath }) into definition/workflow repos, but never removed

the older bare call that now conflicts. The per-path signal drives the

extension's scoped walk which correctly detects absent files for S3

deletion — the bare call was overriding it.

Reproduced: minio + managedConfig. model delete --server left the S3

object orphaned; CLI model delete (two-phase push path) correctly removed

it.

Co-authored-by: Blake Irvin <bixu@users.noreply.github.com>

asserts no bare markDirty() calls, per-path calls from re

Aparse YAML AST with version 1.1 to preserve timestamp string quoting (swamp-club#2120) (#2441)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-13T22:24:03Zpersistencefix

What's Changed

  • fix(persistence): parse YAML AST with version 1.1 to preserve timestamp string quoting (swamp-club#2120) (#2441)

Summary

  • Fix YAML 1.1/1.2 schema mismatch in YamlDefinitionRepository.save() where the comment-preserving AST merge path used npm yaml's default YAML 1.2 schema, causing timestamp-looking strings in replaced sequences to lose their quotes
  • On reload with @std/yaml (YAML 1.1), unquoted timestamps resolved as Date objects, breaking string/enum schema validation
  • Pass { version: "1.1" } to parseDocument() to align AST creation/emission with the @std/yaml read path
  • Add regression test covering timestamp string quoting in sequences with simultaneous comment preservation

Regression introduced in #2265 (924cbecb). Reported by external contributor mgreten.

Closes swamp-club#2120

  • feat(audit): Phase 5 — alerting, HMAC rotation, compliance reports, streaming export, hot-reload (#2438)

Summary

Phase 5 of the serve audit log (#2103). Five capabilities on the existing Phase 1-4 infrastructure:

  • Alert rules: AlertRuleEngine with sliding-window counters, cooldown, recursion guard for system/alert.* events. Webhook + system-event delivery. audit.alerts server request + swamp audit alerts CLI command
