2026-09-11T21:54:58Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): improve token auth diagnostics — vault-aware sweep and structured rejection reasons (swamp-club#2113) (#2436)

Summary

  • The boot-time token consistency sweep only checked _token-secrets for

missing secrets, falsely reporting vault-backed tokens as dead. Now it probes

the token's actual vaultName before declaring missing-secret.

  • Token auth rejection logged a generic "Authentication failed" with no reason

class. Now classifies errors (expired, revoked, secret-mismatch,

vault-error, no-definition) and surfaces the reason in both the daemon

log and the 401 response body.

Summary

YamlDefinitionRepository / YamlWorkflowRepository instances per request.

tracer provider leaked between test files.

causing failures when the verification workflow set the env var.

  • Added architecture fitness test preventing serve handlers from constructing

fresh definition or workflow repos.

Asyslog circuit breaker and UDP message size limit (#2428)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-10T17:50:20Zauditfix

What's Changed

  • fix(audit): syslog circuit breaker and UDP message size limit (#2428)

Summary

Follow-up to #2074 (audit Phase 4) addressing review feedback:

  • Syslog circuit breaker — after 3 failed connection attempts, the sink

skips reconnection for 60s instead of retrying every drain cycle. Prevents

connection storms when the syslog server is down.

  • UDP message truncation — truncate UDP syslog messages to 2048 bytes to

stay within typical MTU limits per RFC 5424 transport recommendations.

Apush workflow and vault mutations to remote datastore under managedConfig (#2424)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-09T13:09:54Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): push workflow and vault mutations to remote datastore under managedConfig (#2424)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2083 — workflow and vault create/edit/delete operations under

managedConfig: true never pushed to the remote datastore, so definitions

vanished on pod restart.

  • Add syncService.markDirty() + pushChanged() blocks to

handleWorkflowCreate, handleWorkflowEdit, handleWorkflowDelete,

handleVaultCreate, handleVaultEdit, and handleVaultDelete — mirroring

the existing working pattern from handleModelCreate/handleModelDelete

  • Fix workflowsDirFor to delegate to resolveEffectiveWorkflowsDir so

broken-workflow scanning uses the managed config path when active

Root Cause

The original managedConfig commit (661f4896) only wired model handlers with

sync calls. Workflow and vault handlers were never updated — they wrote files to

the correct local managed cache path (via the managedConfigRegistry fallback)

but never called syncService.markDirty() + pushChanged() to push them to

S3/GCS.

Reproduction

Confirmed with minio + swamp serve:

  • swamp model create → persisted to S3, survived restart ✓
  • swamp workflow create → missing from S3, lost on restart ✗
  • swamp vault create → missing from S3, lost on restart ✗

After fix, all three persist correctly.

  • [x] All 11418 unit tests pass (0 failed)
  • [x] Type check, lint, fmt pass
  • [x] Compile + binary check pass
  • [x] Code review:

Aforce UTF-8 output encoding in PowerShellStrategy (swamp-club#2070) (#2416)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-09T00:02:49Zshellfix

What's Changed

  • fix(shell): force UTF-8 output encoding in PowerShellStrategy (swamp-club#2070) (#2416)

Summary

  • Force UTF-8 output encoding in PowerShellStrategy.buildInvocation() by prepending [Console]::OutputEncoding and $OutputEncoding assignments to every PowerShell command
  • Fixes Windows model output containing UTF-16LE null-byte-interleaved text instead of UTF-8

Root Cause

PowerShellStrategy wraps commands with powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command <cmd> but never configured output encoding. Windows PowerShell 5.1 defaults to the system OEM code page (or UTF-16LE for piped output). The TextDecoder in process_executor.ts always decodes as UTF-8, producing garbled text with null bytes interleaved between characters.

Changes

  • src/domain/models/command/shell/powershell_strategy.ts — Add UTF8_ENCODING_PREFIX constant and prepend it in buildInvocation()
  • src/domain/models/command/shell/powershell_strategy_test.ts — Update both buildInvocation tests to assert UTF-8 prefix is present

Test plan

  • [x] PowerShell strategy unit tests pass (6/6)
  • [x] All shell model tests pass (36 passed, 20 ignored — PowerShell-only tests that only run on Windows)
  • [x] Full verification workflow: lint, fmt, type-check, tests, compile, code review, adversarial review all pass
  • [ ] Windows CI: 5 failing vault roundtrip UAT tests in swamp-uat should pass with this fix

Fixes swamp-club#2070

Aadd src/cli/ to adversarial review guard (swamp-club#2052) (#2401)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-07T22:51:24Zverificationfix

What's Changed

  • fix(verification): add src/cli/ to adversarial review guard (swamp-club#2052) (#2401)

Summary

  • Add f.startsWith('src/cli/') to the adversarial review guard in verification/workflow-verify-reviews.yaml so CLI infrastructure files (auth_context.ts, auto_resolver_adapters.ts, control_plane_vault.ts, load_identity.ts, repo_context.ts, resolve_datastore.ts) receive adversarial review coverage
  • Update the guard table in agent-constraints/verification-conventions.md to match

Context

The adversarial review guard only matched src/domain/, src/infrastructure/, src/libswamp/, src/serve/, src/worker/. Files in src/cli/ outside src/cli/commands/ — which contain security-relevant infrastructure code — skipped adversarial review entirely. This was an original omission when the guard was introduced in commit 66f1a629.

2026-09-07T18:14:13Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(access): filter search/list results by grant scope instead of rejecting on wildcard (swamp-club#2042) (#2391)

Summary

  • Search/list handlers checked authorization against a hardcoded wildcard

resource (name: "*"), requiring callers to hold a global grant like

denied entirely.

accessible items are returned.

hasAnyGrantForKind check — any read grant for the resource kind is

sufficient.

  • Admin users with explicit deny grants are correctly denied on matching

items (deny wins over admin fallback, matching authorizeOrReject

semantics).

  • Projected data.query (with select) and summarise keep the original

wildcard gate since their results can't be meaningfully filtered per-item.

  • AccessDecisionService: Added hasAnyGrantForKind(principal, action, kind) — returns true if the principal has at least one allow grant for the kind+action
  • shared.ts: Added filterByAuthorization() (per-item filtering with admin fallback respecting deny grants) and authorizeAnyOrReject() (coarse gate with audit emission)
  • 13 handlers updated across model_handlers.ts, workflow_handlers.ts, data_handlers.ts

Araise sync error message truncation limit from 200 to 2000 chars (#2041) (#2385)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-07T06:06:25Zdatastoresfix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastores): raise sync error message truncation limit from 200 to 2000 chars (#2041) (#2385)

Summary

  • Raise MESSAGE_PREVIEW_LIMIT in sync_error_diagnostic.ts from 200 to 2000 chars
  • Update truncation test to match the new limit

Problem

When extension datastores (e.g. @swamp/s3-datastore) throw errors that list

failing file paths, the 200-char truncation budget is consumed by file paths

before the actual S3/MinIO error (e.g. AccessDenied, SignatureDoesNotMatch)

is shown. Users see a truncated message with no actionable root cause.

Discovered while diagnosing a user's datastore setup extension push failure

where 24901 files failed — the warning showed only file paths, hiding the

actual error from the extension.

Fix

Bump the limit to 2000 chars so the actual error detail is visible in the

warning output. The full untruncated message remains available on

fields.errorMessage for consumers that need fidelity.

Test plan

  • [x] Updated existing truncation test to assert on 2000-char limit
  • [x] All 17 sync_error_diagnostic_test.ts tests pass
  • [x] Full verification: lint, fmt, type-check, tests, compile, code review,

adversarial review

Closes swamp-club/lab#2041

Ause atomic rename for embedded deno binary extraction to prevent ETXTBSY (swamp-club#2038) (#2382)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-07T04:24:54Zruntimefix

What's Changed

  • fix(runtime): use atomic rename for embedded deno binary extraction to prevent ETXTBSY (swamp-club#2038) (#2382)

Aclean up unreadable lock files instead of timing out (swamp-club#2039) (#2381)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-07T04:17:24Zlockfix

What's Changed

  • fix(lock): clean up unreadable lock files instead of timing out (swamp-club#2039) (#2381)

Summary

  • Fix FileLock.acquire() to treat unreadable lock files (0 bytes or corrupt JSON) as stale, removing them and retrying instead of spinning until the 60-second timeout
  • Add unit tests for zero-byte and corrupt-JSON lock file recovery

Problem

When a process crashes between Deno.open({ createNew: true }) and file.write(), the lock file is left as 0 bytes on disk. readLockFile() returns null because JSON.parse("") throws, and the if (existing) guard skips the staleness check entirely — causing the acquire loop to back off and retry until timeout, never cleaning up the orphaned file.

Reported by @svendowideit after a reboot left a stale lock from July 31st blocking workflow runs.

Fix

Added an else branch after the existing staleness check: when the lock file exists on disk but readLockFile() returns null, treat it as stale (no holder info to respect), log a warning, remove it, and retry.

Test plan

  • [x] New test: zero-byte lock file is cleaned up and acquire succeeds
  • [x] New test: corrupt JSON lock file is cleaned up and acquire succeeds
  • [x] All 22 existing file_lock_test.ts tests pass
  • [x] Full verification: lint, fmt, type-check, tests, compile, code review, adversarial review

Closes swamp-club/lab#2039

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-04T18:28:01Zaccessfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(access): add approve action for workflow gate authorization (#2378)

Summary

  • Adds approve to ActionSchema so workflow.approve and workflow.reject can be authorized independently from workflow.run
  • run implies approve for backwards compatibility — existing grants keep working unchanged
  • Organizations that want separation of duties can now grant approve without run, enabling approve-only roles

Details

The grant model previously had four actions: run, read, write, admin. Both workflow.approve and workflow.reject handlers authorized against run, making it impossible to express "may approve a gate but may not start runs." What changed:

  • ActionSchema extended with approve
  • grantMatchesAction treats run as implying approve
  • handleWorkflowApprove and handleWorkflowReject authorize against approve instead of run
  • handleWorkflowResume stays on run (resume is execution, not a gate decision)
  • CLI help strings and error messages updated
  • access_helpers.ts now derives valid actions from ActionSchema.options instead of hardcoding
  • Design docs updated (access-control.md, workflows.md)

What's NOT in scope:

  • No four-eyes / self-approval prevention logic
  • No run.initiatedBy in CEL conditions
  • Self-app

Awiden isUuid() to accept all RFC UUID versions (swamp-club#1999) (#2375)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-04T16:28:53Zmodelsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(models): widen isUuid() to accept all RFC UUID versions (swamp-club#1999) (#2375)

Summary

  • Widen UUID_PATTERN regex from v4-only (4[0-9a-f]{3}) to versions 1-8 ([1-8][0-9a-f]{3}) in all 5 files where it is defined
  • Fixes debug log flooding in getContent/findAllForModel for deterministic UUIDv5 model IDs
  • Fixes incorrect name-based routing in resolveModelId and workflow operations for non-v4 UUIDs

Details

isUuid() hardcoded the version nibble to 4, rejecting valid v1/v3/v5/v7/v8 UUIDs. This caused @hivemq/asdlc's deterministic UUIDv5 model IDs (uuidv5(sha1("<type>/<name>"))) to trip the "called with model name instead of UUID" debug warning on every read, and to route through findByName instead of findById.

The fix widens the version nibble to [1-8] (covering RFC 4122 v1-5 and RFC 9562 v6-8) while preserving the RFC variant bits check ([89ab]). Nil UUID (v0) and reserved versions (9+) remain excluded.

|------|--------|

| src/domain/models/model_lookup.ts | Widen pattern, update JSDoc |

| src/libswamp/workflows/get.ts | Widen pattern, update JSDoc |

| src/libswamp/workflows/delete.ts | Widen pattern |

| src/libswamp/workflows/edit.ts | Widen pattern, update JSDoc |

| src/domain/models/model_lookup_test.ts | Add v1/v3/v5/v7 positive tests, fix negative tests |

Askip write locks for non-mutating model method invocations (#2374)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-04T00:22:24Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): skip write locks for non-mutating model method invocations (#2374)

Summary

  • Skip acquireModelLocks for non-mutating model methods (read/list kind) in both serve handlers and the CLI path
  • Guard the fallback sync push (markDirty + pushChanged) to also skip for read methods
  • Add isMethodMutating helper that resolves the model type definition and checks method kind via inferMethodKind/isMutatingKind, with safe fallback to mutating on resolution failure

Fixes swamp-club#1992

Details

model_handlers.ts called acquireModelLocks on every method invocation, including pure-read methods like status. This caused concurrent read-only calls to block behind in-progress writes on the same model instance. The domain already distinguishes read from write methods via MethodKind and isMutatingKind() — this change wires that into the lock acquisition decision.

Methods with kind: "read" or kind: "list" (either explicit or name-inferred) now skip lock acquisition entirely. Unknown method kinds default to mutating (safe).

  • Add hydrateFile to RepositoryContext interface so construction sites can access it

Ause instanceId for PID namespace locality in containers (swamp-club#1958) (#2357)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-02T18:09:27Zrun-trackerfix

What's Changed

  • fix(run-tracker): use instanceId for PID namespace locality in containers (swamp-club#1958) (#2357)

Summary

  • Bug: reapStaleRuns and reapDeadProcessRuns used hostname() to determine PID namespace locality. In Docker, multiple containers sharing a hostname but with separate PID namespaces caused the liveness check to incorrectly identify foreign-container rows as local — stale records accumulated indefinitely.
  • Fix: Both reap methods now accept an optional instanceId parameter. When provided (serve context), instanceId is used instead of hostname for PID namespace discrimination. Falls back to hostname for CLI callers and legacy rows without instanceId.
  • Mirrors the pattern already used in serve boot reconciliation (serve.ts:562-578).

Test plan

  • [x] 8 new unit tests covering all instanceId-aware branches: same/different instance for both reap methods, legacy rows, and no-instanceId parameter fallback

Areplace broken model-method sweep with direct repo writes in boot reconciliation (swamp-club#1955) (#2352)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-02T17:31:52Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): replace broken model-method sweep with direct repo writes in boot reconciliation (swamp-club#1955) (#2352)

Summary

  • sweepStaleRecords ran a full modelMethodRun per stale lease (~12.5s each) to flip a state field. readResource is scoped to the current definition UUID, but when the auto-definition was recreated (ephemeral filesystem + persistent data in a remote datastore), the UUID diverged — causing 100% expiry failure and a multi-minute boot stall.
  • Replace the transition-based sweep with direct repo.save() writes using the actual filesystem modelId from loadAttrsForType. All three sweep types (leases, pending dispatches, workers) are fixed.
  • Worker sweep replicates the full setStatus field-update logic (clearing activeDispatchIds, setting disconnectedAt, clearing verifyFailureReason, updating lastSeenAt).
  • Removed defaultRunTransition, TransitionInput, runTransition from BootReconciliationDeps, and unused libswamp imports.

Reported by @magistr — thank you for the thorough diagnosis.

Related

  • Fixes swamp-club#1955
  • Filed swamp-club#1958 for the separate PID namespace collision in run tracker

Reproduction

Verified with MinIO as S3-compatible datastore:

1. Init repo with @swamp/s3-datastore pointing at MinIO

2. Acquire active leases, sync to MinIO

3. Delete local managed cache (simulating container restart)

4. Start serve — data hydrates from MinIO under old UUID

Aappend OTLP signal path to URL pathname, not after query/fragment (swamp-club#1716) (#2350)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-02T16:06:22Ztracingfix

What's Changed

  • fix(tracing): append OTLP signal path to URL pathname, not after query/fragment (swamp-club#1716) (#2350)

Summary

  • Fix resolveOtlpEndpoint to use the URL constructor instead of string concatenation, so /v1/<signal> is appended to the pathname rather than after query parameters or fragments
  • Add tests for query-only, fragment-only, and combined query+fragment OTLP endpoint URLs

Problem

Generic OTLP endpoint construction appended /v1/<signal> to the raw URL string. An endpoint like https://collector.example/otlp?token=secret became https://collector.example/otlp?token=secret/v1/traces — the signal suffix landed in the query instead of the URL path.

Fix

Parse the endpoint with the URL constructor, modify pathname, and reconstruct via toString(). This preserves query and fragment components in their correct positions.

  • Added 3 new unit tests covering query, fragment, and combined query+fragment endpoints
  • All 19 tests in otel_config_test.ts pass (16 existing + 3 new)

  • The dashboard's ApprovalInfo interface expected grouped entries with a nested steps array, but workflow.approvals has always returned flat PendingApproval entries (one per suspended step)

Asupport env and vault expressions in datastore config (swamp-club#1801) (#2340)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-01T22:17:36Zdatastorefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(datastore): support env and vault expressions in datastore config (swamp-club#1801) (#2340)

Summary

  • Add ${{ env.VAR }} and ${{ vault.get(name, key) }} expression resolution to datastore config values in .swamp.yaml
  • Uses the same expression delimiter syntax as model/workflow definitions for consistency
  • VaultService is lazily created via a lightweight early-boot factory that reads local vault configs without triggering the full vault type registry loading
  • All installed vault types work (native local_encryption and extension providers like @swamp/aws-sm)

Details

Datastore config in .swamp.yaml previously required literal token values. This forced operators to choose between committing secrets, gitignoring .swamp.yaml, or using git skip-worktree hacks. This change adds expression resolution at the datastore config layer, applied during resolveDatastoreConfig() before schema validation and createProvider().

The resolver (src/cli/datastore_expression_resolver.ts) recursively walks config objects and resolves:

  • ${{ env.VAR_NAME }} — via Deno.env.get()
  • ${{ vault.get(vaultName, secretKey) }} — via a lazy VaultService

Four integration points in resolve_datastore.ts cover both .swamp.yaml and SWAMP_DATASTORE env var paths. The managedConfig circular dependency is detected and produces a clear error.

Test plan

  • [x] 23 unit tests for the expression resolver (env, vault, mixed, errors, edge cases)

Ashow remote mode indicator on server-targeted commands (swamp-club#1933) (#2336)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-01T18:01:53Zclifeat

What's Changed

  • feat(cli): show remote mode indicator on server-targeted commands (swamp-club#1933) (#2336)

Summary

When a command dispatches to a remote swamp serve instance, a Remote <url> indicator line is now written to stderr before the first network I/O. This makes it immediately obvious that the command is targeting a server rather than the local repository — preventing accidental remote operations when SWAMP_SERVE_URL or SWAMP_SERVER_URL is set.

The indicator is centralized in the two remote dispatch entry points (streamServerRun and requestServerResponse) in src/cli/remote_run.ts, so all remote-capable commands get it automatically without touching any individual command files.

Changes

  • Added writeRemoteIndicator() helper using the existing gutter-line format with warn color
  • Called from streamServerRun (covers all streaming commands) and requestServerResponse (covers all request-response commands)
  • 2 unit tests for format and URL inclusion
  • Updated design/enablers/remote-execution.md to document the indicator

  • Verified the indicator appears in existing streaming and request-response test output

Bskip the model-definition scan when no expression needs it (swamp-club#2123) (#2468)

`perf` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T16:37:55Zexpressionsperf

What's Changed

  • perf(expressions): skip the model-definition scan when no expression needs it (swamp-club#2123) (#2468)

Summary

Building an expression context used to always load every model definition in the repository, even when no expression referenced model or file. Now requiresModelNamespace() checks each definition or workflow and uses the existing light context when it can.

  • Expression analysis: requiresModelNamespace() in dependency_extractor.ts uses a deliberately broad pattern, /(?<![.\w])(?:model|file)\b/. A false positive only costs the scan that used to happen anyway. The pattern also catches the bare namespace used as a value (has(model), size(file)). It skips properties that just share the name (inputs.model, data.model.x).
  • Call sites:
  • ExpressionEvaluationService.evaluateDefinition() picks light or full context. evaluateAllDefinitions() loads definitions once and passes them to ModelResolver.buildContext().
  • WorkflowExecutionService run and resume check the workflow YAML, then the stored definitions of the workflow's own model-method steps. A dynamic modelIdOrName forces the full context.
  • swamp workflow evaluate applies the same check to the workflow YAML.
  • Same results on both paths: when the lookup table isn't there, data.latest() falls back to getLatestRecord(). That fallback now resolves sensitive vault refs, as the other path already did. It also loads text content for text/* data,

Breuse name→path hints and skip impossible ID scans (#2466)

`perf` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-16T00:15:29Zpersistenceperf

What's Changed

  • perf(persistence): reuse name→path hints and skip impossible ID scans (#2466)

Summary

Repeated targeted lookups re-walked and re-parsed unrelated YAML definitions, and several callers threw away identity information they already held (swamp-club#2124).

  • Gate the exact-ID fallbacks on UUID syntax. findDefinitionByIdOrName and ModelResolver.resolveModel fell back to an exact-equality ID search after a name miss regardless of input syntax. A non-UUID string can never match that comparison, but reaching it parsed every definition in the repository — once per registered model type. Partial-ID matching (matchByPartialId) is untouched.
  • Add name→path hints to YamlDefinitionRepository and YamlWorkflowRepository.findByName. Definitions stored under a legacy {uuid}.yaml filename, or under a name that is not filename-safe, miss the name-derived fast path and re-walk the repo on every lookup. The hints store a path only — never a parsed definition, never a negative result — and are validated on every read by re-parsing the file and confirming its declared name still matches. That validate-on-read rule keeps external edits, renames, additions and deletions visible, and is why save() and delete() need no invalidation bookkeeping.
  • Scope workflow report assembly to the step's own model type. The model_resolved event carries the resolved definition's own name and type together, so report assembly calls findByName(type, name) inste

Adds scheduled flaky-test detection (swamp-club#2282). .github/workflows/flaky-tests.yml runs deno task test --repeats 3 on ubuntu-latest and macos-latest every 6 hours, and on workflow_dispatch.

It only detects. It has no push or pull_request trigger and is not a required check, so it cannot gate a merge. verify-build stays strict with no --retry, so a PR that adds a flaky test cannot pass on retry and get attested.

How it works

  • Test job: matrix of Linux and macOS with fail-fast: false, a 60-minute timeout and contents: read. It runs only in swamp-club/swamp, never on forks, and runs never overlap. setup-deno is SHA-pinned and reads .tool-versions. The test step sets NO_COLOR, and on failure the run summary lists Deno's FAILURES block for that OS. If the run dies before Deno prints it (OOM, timeout, type check), the summary shows the last 50 lines of the log instead.
  • Alert job: runs on failure with permissions: {}, because it talks only to swamp-club. This repo tracks issues in the lab (auto-response.yml moves every GitHub issue there), so the alert posts to the lab directly using SWAMP_CLUB_API_KEY. It searches for an open issue titled "Scheduled flaky-test detection is failing" (shipped and closed issues are skipped). If one exists it comments with the run link; otherwise it opens a new one. Amake .tool-versions the single source of truth for Deno (swamp-club#1964) (#2511)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-18T14:21:25Zbuildfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(build): make .tool-versions the single source of truth for Deno (swamp-club#1964) (#2511)

Summary

Closes swamp-club#1964.

Deno has no native per-project version pinning, so this repo carried eight independent pins with nothing keeping them in step: six CI steps on a floating v2.9.x, the Dockerfile on an exact tag, and the dead verification container still on 2.8.3.

.tool-versions at the repo root is now the single source of truth. mise reads it natively, and denoland/setup-deno reads the *same file* via its deno-version-file input — already available in the SHA-pinned v2.0.4 the publish workflows use. CI keeps its existing action and no new third-party action enters the supply chain; a .mise.toml would have forced jdx/mise-action onto every runner, which is worst on windows-compat.yml. Nothing in CI executes mise — mise is a local developer convenience only.

Why this is more than developer convenience

scripts/download_deno.ts derives the embedded runtime version from deno --version on the building machine and bakes that exact release into the compiled binary for user extensions. With release.yml on a floating v2.9.x, consecutive releases could ship different embedded Deno patches with nothing recording which. Pinning exactly makes the shipped artifact reproducible.

The script now also guards that at its source: it compares the running Deno against the pin, failing hard under CI and warning locally. Th

Docs changed: README.md

What broke (PR #2425)

1. claude-review removed as redundant (reviews now run locally)

2. No other job provides the PR approval

3. auto-merge fails with "the base branch policy prohibits the merge"

  • Add Windows PowerShell installation instructions to the release body

Details

This PR wires the Windows target into the release pipeline.

The .exe is compiled to /tmp/ and zipped into dist/ so only the .zip appears in dist/ — the sha256sum swamp-* glob captures it cleanly alongside the bare Linux/macOS binaries.

The smoke test job runs with permissions: contents: read (least-privilege) and checks $LASTEXITCODE after --version to catch PowerShell's native-command exit-code swallowing.

S3 upload to artifacts.swamp-club.com is out of scope — no existing platform does S3 uploads in release.yml.

Cdescribe the three bundle-cache cases after #2354 (swamp-club#2372) (#2557)

`docs` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-22T21:10:57Zdesigndocs

What's Changed

  • docs(design): describe the three bundle-cache cases after #2354 (swamp-club#2372) (#2557)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#2372. Docs-only.

PR #2553 (swamp-club#2354) changed when the extension loader warns about cached bundles, but the "Fingerprint preservation on build failure (issue #265)" paragraph in design/primitives/extensions.md still described the old behaviour: a single fromCache fallback whose warning fired on fromCache && newFingerprint !== catalogFingerprint.

The paragraph now describes the BundleResult cacheReason union and the single warning each case produces:

| Cache reason | bundleWithCache | Reconcile (rebundleAndUpdateCatalog) |

|---|---|---|

| trusted-pulled | reuses the pulled bundle, no rebundle attempted | debug |

| rebundle-failed, expectedFailure: false | warn Rebundle failed for <file>, using cached bundle: <error> | debug |

| rebundle-failed, expectedFailure: true | debug | warn Bundle could not be regenerated … will retry on next command |

It also states that fingerprint preservation is identical in all three cases. Two details are stated precisely:

  • An expected failure is defined by isExpectedBundleFailure, which checks that no project deno.json/deno.jsonc exists; bare specifiers are the typical cause.
  • A pulled extension is trusted-pulled only when its bundle exists and either trustPulledCache is set or the failure would be expected.

The rationale for fingerprint preservatio

Cdocument how to verify TTY-only behaviour with a pty (#2508)

`docs` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T23:23:31Zverificationdocs

What's Changed

  • docs(verification): document how to verify TTY-only behaviour with a pty (#2508)

Summary

Verifying swamp-club#2254 turned on a code path an agent shell cannot reach. prettyOutput is !noColor && isStdinTty(), so a TTY session uses the pretty sink and a scripted run uses the plain console sink — and the bug lived in the path a script never touches. The plan for that issue recorded the case as "needs a human at a terminal," which is the kind of note that makes a whole class of bugs look unverifiable.

It isn't. script supplies a real pty on stdin while stdout and stderr still redirect where you point them, so the interactive path is testable in the same automated run and the two streams stay separable.

Adds a "Verifying TTY-Only Behaviour" section to agent-constraints/verification-conventions.md covering:

  • why the path exists, with initializeLogging as the live example
  • the script invocation, and cat -A so a stray blank line is visible
  • asserting tty inside the subshell, so a harness that silently drops the pty can't make the check pass vacuously
  • running the same harness against the released binary as well as the built one — two runs differing only in the binary are what separate a real fix from a test that was always going to pass
  • the other things this covers: prompts from prompt_helpers.ts, Ink components, anything branching on Deno.stdout.isTerminal()

Closes swamp-club#2266.

Test Plan

Docs only — no source chang

𝗗 release v20260917.232152.0-sha.4430c4b1𝗖𝗟 4430c4b1𝗣 #2508𝗔 lab#2254

Ccorrect shard index push path and cleanup semantics (#2506)

`docs` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-17T22:05:54Zdatastoresdocs

What's Changed

  • docs(datastores): correct shard index push path and cleanup semantics (#2506)

Summary

design/enablers/datastores.md still documented the pre-fix shard-first index

behaviour that caused swamp-club lab#2245. Two sections were wrong:

  • Push path (commitPush) — framed as read-merge-write "under the global

lock". Serve-handler pushes take the same path without holding the lock, so

the lock never serialized these writes: a writer with a stale view rewrote the

shard from its own entries and silently dropped everyone else's. Now documents

the compare-and-swap merge (etag/generation conditional PUT) that every v2

index write actually performs.

  • Shard cleanup — said an emptied shard is deleted from _index/ and its key

removed from _meta.json. The shipped fix deliberately does the opposite: the

shard is left in place as an empty object, because S3-compatible stores ignore

If-Match on DELETE and a delete would race a concurrent add.

The behaviour shipped in swamp-extensions#290; that PR called out the core-repo

doc update as needing its own PR. Left as-is, the doc instructs the next

datastore-extension author to rebuild the same data-loss bug.

Docs only — one file, no code, config, or test changes.

Closes swamp-club lab#2256

Cwrite enablers/access-control.md design doc (swamp-club#1925) (#2332)

`docs` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-09-01T01:27:07Zdocs

What's Changed

  • docs: write enablers/access-control.md design doc (swamp-club#1925) (#2332)

Summary

  • Write design/enablers/access-control.md documenting the access control subsystem that enables the serve primitive: principals, subjects/groups, grant schema and sources, the deny-first evaluation model, workflow execution context authorization, the can-i request flow, and admission
  • Update design/README.md to add the new enabler to the index table
  • Convert the forward reference in design/primitives/serve.md to a link to the now-existing doc

Why

A customer asked whether workflow-internal model method calls are authorized against the caller's model grants or the workflow grant. The answer (workflow grant is sufficient) is correct by design but was not documented anywhere, leading to confusion and overly broad grant configurations.
